and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\bDiv_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bGradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}}

with associated domain complexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\symRot)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mbH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\devGrad)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bGrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\devGrad)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mbH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Div)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mbH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Rot)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mbH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Gradgrad)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\bDiv_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bGradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}}

Here we have introduced the densely defined and closed linear operators

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto\Gradgrad u,

\displaystyle\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}:D(\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto\divDiv S,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto\Rot S,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\symRot T,

\displaystyle\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:D(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\Div T,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bGrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bGrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\mapsto\devGrad v,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\mapsto\devGrad v,

\displaystyle\bDiv_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}:D(\bDiv_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\Div T,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\symRot T,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto\Rot S,

\displaystyle\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:D(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto\divDiv S,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bGradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bGradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto\Gradgrad u,

with domains of definition

\displaystyle D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bGrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\mbH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\devGrad,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\devGrad,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\bDiv_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Div,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bGradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\mbH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega).

Moreover,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto\Gradgrad u,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bGradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bGradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto\Gradgrad u,

\displaystyle\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}:D(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto\divDiv S,

\displaystyle\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1}:D(\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1})\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega);

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto\divDiv S,

with domains of definition

\displaystyle D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bGradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1})

\displaystyle=\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1})

\displaystyle=\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega).

2.6.
Dirichlet/Neumann Fields

We also introduce the cohomology spaces of
biharmonic Dirichlet/Neumann tensor fields (generalised harmonic tensors)

\displaystyle\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)

\displaystyle:=N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})\cap N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)=\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\varepsilon^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)

\displaystyle:=N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}})\cap N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}\mu)=\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\symRot,\Omega)\cap\mu^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega).

Here,
\varepsilon:\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)\to\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)

is a symmetric and positive
topological isomorphism (symmetric and positive bijective bounded linear operator),
which introduces a new inner product

\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}:=\langle\varepsilon\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)},

where
\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega):=\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)
(as linear space)
equipped with the inner product
\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}
.
Such
weights

\varepsilon
and also
\mu:\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)\to\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)

are called
admissible
.
Typical examples are given by
symmetric,
\sfL^{\infty}
-bounded, and uniformly positive definite tensor fields

\varepsilon,\mu:\Omega\to\reals^{(3\times 3)\times(3\times 3)}

with appropriate algebraic properties.

3.
Biharmonic Complexes II

3.1.
Regular Potentials and Decompositions I

3.1.1.
Extendable Domains

The next theorem is a crucial result.
Its proof is based on
[
6
, Theorem 3.10]
, where the stated results
for
\Gamma_{\!t}=\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{\!t}=\emptyset
have been shown,
and the arguments used in, e.g.,
[
3
, Lemma 4.4]

for partial boundary conditions.
See Appendix
C
for a detailed proof.

Theorem 3.1

(regular potential operators for extendable domains)
.

Let
(\Omega,\Gamma_{\!t})
be an extendable bounded strong Lipschitz pair
and let
k\geq 0
. Then there exist bounded linear regular potential operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k+2}(\reals^{3}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k+1}(\reals^{3}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k+1}(\reals^{3}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k+1}(\reals^{3}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k+1}(\reals^{3}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k+2}(\reals^{3}).

In particular,
\mathcal{P}_{\dots}^{\dots}
are right inverses for

\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}
,
\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}}
,
\Div_{\mathbb{T}}
,
\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}
,
\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}}
, and
\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}}
, respectively, i.e.,

\displaystyle\Gradgrad\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\devGrad\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\Rot\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\symRot\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\Div\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id_{\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}},

\displaystyle\divDiv\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id_{\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}}.

Without loss of generality,
\mathcal{P}_{\dots}^{\dots}

map to tensor fields with a fixed compact support in
\reals^{3}
.

Remark 3.2
.

Note that
\A_{n}\mathcal{P}_{\A_{n}}=\id_{R(\A_{n})}
is a general property
of a (bounded regular) potential operator
\mathcal{P}_{\A_{n}}:R(\A_{n})\to\sfH^{+}_{n}

with
\sfH^{+}_{n}\subset D(\A_{n})
, cf.
[
3
, Section 2.3]
.

As a simple consequence of the complex properties,
the general results for regular potentials and decompositions
from, e.g.,
[
3
, Section 2.3]
, and Theorem
3.1

we obtain a few corollaries.

Corollary 3.3

(regular potentials for extendable domains)
.

Let
(\Omega,\Gamma_{\!t})
be an extendable bounded strong Lipschitz pair
and let
k\geq 0
. Then the regular potentials representations

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Gradgrad\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)=\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Rot\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)=\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=\Div\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)=\Div\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\devGrad\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\devGrad,\Omega)=\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\symRot\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)=\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=\divDiv\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)=\divDiv\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\divDiv\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})

hold, and the potentials can be chosen such that they depend continuously on the data.
In particular, the latter spaces are closed subspaces of

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)
,
\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)
, and
\sfH^{k}(\Omega)
, respectively.

Corollary 3.4

(regular decompositions for extendable domains)
.

Let
(\Omega,\Gamma_{\!t})
be an extendable bounded strong Lipschitz pair
and let
k\geq 0
. Then the bounded regular decompositions

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)=R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\Gradgrad R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)=R(\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\Rot R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)=R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\devGrad R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)=R(\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\dotplus\symRot R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

hold with bounded linear regular decomposition operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}\Rot:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:=\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}(1-\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}):\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}\Div:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:=\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}(1-\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}):\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}\symRot:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:=\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}(1-\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}):\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}\divDiv:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:=\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}(1-\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}):\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

satisfying

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\Gradgrad\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\Rot\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\devGrad\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\symRot\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)}.

Remark 3.5
.

Note that for (bounded linear) potential operators
\mathcal{P}_{\A_{n-1}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\A_{n}}
the identity

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{1}+\A_{n-1}\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\id_{D(\A_{n})}\quad\text{with}

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\A_{n}}\A_{n}:D(\A_{n})

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{+}_{n},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{0}:=\mathcal{P}_{\A_{n-1}}(1-\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{1}):D(\A_{n})

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{+}_{n-1}

is a general structure of a (bounded) regular decomposition. Moreover:

(i)

R(\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{1})=R(\mathcal{P}_{\A_{n}})

and
R(\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{0})=R(\mathcal{P}_{\A_{n-1}})
.

(ii)

N(\A_{n})
is invariant under
\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{1}
,
as
\A_{n}=\A_{n}\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{1}
holds by the complex property.

(iii)

\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{1}
and
\A_{n-1}\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{0}=1-\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{1}

are projections.

(iv)

There exists
c>0
such that for all
x\in D(\A_{n})

|\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{1}x|_{\sfH^{+}_{n}}\leq c|\A_{n}x|_{\sfH_{n+1}}.

(iv’)

In particular,
\mathcal{Q}_{\A_{n}}^{1}|_{N(\A_{n})}=0
.

Corollary 3.6

(weak and strong partial boundary conditions coincide for extendable domains)
.

Let
(\Omega,\Gamma_{\!t})
be an extendable bounded strong Lipschitz pair
and let
k\geq 0
. Then weak and strong boundary conditions coincide, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)=\mbH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\devGrad,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\devGrad,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)=\mbH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega).

Similar versions of Corollary
3.4

and Corollary
3.6

are available for the non-standard Sobolev spaces
of the form
\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\dots}(\cdots,\Omega)
,
cf. Section
2.4
.
Note that

(7)

\displaystyle\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

as

\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)\subset\mbH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\subset\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)
.

Corollary 3.7

(Corollary
3.4
and Corollary
3.6

for non-standard Sobolev spaces)
.

Let
(\Omega,\Gamma_{\!t})
be an extendable bounded strong Lipschitz pair
and let
k\geq 1
. Then the bounded regular decompositions

\displaystyle\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)=R(\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k-1})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k-1})\dotplus\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k-1})\dotplus\symRot R(\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

hold with bounded linear regular decomposition operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k-1}\divDiv:\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,0}:=\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}(1-\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,1}):\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

satisfying

\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,1}+\symRot\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,0}=\id_{\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)}
.
In particular, weak and strong boundary conditions coincide also for the non-standard Sobolev spaces.

Recall the Hilbert complexes and cohomology groups from Section
2.5
and Section
2.6
.

Theorem 3.8

(closed and exact Hilbert complexes for extendable domains)
.

Let
(\Omega,\Gamma_{\!t})
be an extendable bounded strong Lipschitz pair
and let
k\geq 0
. Both biharmonic domain complexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\symRot)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}}

and, for
k\geq 1
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\symRot)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}}

are exact and closed Hilbert complexes.
In particular, all ranges are closed,
all cohomology groups (Dirichlet/Neumann fields) are trivial,
and the operators from Theorem
3.1

are associated bounded regular potential operators.

3.1.2.
General Strong Lipschitz Domains

From now on we drop the additional condition “extendable domain”, thus

(\Omega,\Gamma_{\!t})
is a bounded strong Lipschitz pair.

Lemma 3.9

(cutting lemma)
.

Let
\varphi\in\sfC^{\infty}(\reals^{3})
and let
k\geq 0
.

(i)

If
S\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)
, then

\varphi S\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)
and

\Rot(\varphi S)=\varphi\Rot S-S\spn\grad\varphi
.

(ii)

If
T\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)
, then

\varphi T\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)
and

\Div(\varphi T)=\varphi\Div T+T\grad\varphi
.

(iii)

If
T\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)
, then

\varphi T\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)
and

\symRot(\varphi T)=\varphi\symRot T-\sym(T\spn\grad\varphi)
.

(iv)

If
k\geq 1
and
S\in\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
, then

\varphi S\in\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
and

\displaystyle\divDiv(\varphi S)=\varphi\divDiv S+2\grad\varphi\cdot\Div S+\Grad\grad\varphi:S.

In particular, this holds for
S\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
.
Note that
\,\cdot\,
and
\,:\,
denotes the point-wise scalar product
for vectors fields and tensor (matrix) fields, respectively.

We proceed by showing crucial regular decompositions for the biharmonic complexes
extending the results of Corollary
3.4

and Corollary
3.7

to our general setting.
The proof is based on Corollary
3.4

together with a partition of unity.

Lemma 3.10

(regular decompositions)
.

Let
k\geq 0
. Then the bounded regular decompositions

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

and, for
k\geq 1
, the non-standard bounded regular decompositions

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)\subset\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

hold with bounded linear regular decomposition operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,1}:\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,0}:\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

satisfying

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\Gradgrad\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\Rot\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\devGrad\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\symRot\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,1}+\symRot\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,0}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)},\qquad k\geq 1.

It holds

\Rot\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}=\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,

\Div\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}=\bDiv_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
, and

\symRot\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}=\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

and thus

\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)
,

\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)
, and

\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

are invariant under

\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}
,

\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}
, and

\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}
, respectively.
Analogously, we have

\divDiv\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}=\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

and
\divDiv\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,1}=\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}

and thus
\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

is invariant under
\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}

and
\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,1}
, respectively.

Corollary
3.6
and (
7
)
are generalised to the following important result.

Corollary 3.11

(weak and strong partial boundary conditions coincide)
.

Let
k\geq 0
. Weak and strong boundary conditions coincide, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)=\mbH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)=\mbH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),\qquad k\geq 1,

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\devGrad,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\devGrad,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)=\mbH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega),\qquad k\geq 1.

In particular, we have
\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bGradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}=\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,
\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}=\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,

\bDiv_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}=\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,
\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\bGrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}=\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,
\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bRot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}=\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,

\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}=\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
, as well as, for
k\geq 1
,

\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\bGradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}=\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}
and
\bdivDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}=\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1}
.

For a detailed proof of Lemma
3.10
and Corollary
3.11

see Appendix
C
.

Theorem 3.12

(compact embeddings)
.

The embeddings

\displaystyle D(\A_{1})\cap D(\A_{0}^{*})=\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\varepsilon^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega),

\displaystyle D(\A_{2})\cap D(\A_{1}^{*})=\mu^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)

are compact. Moreover, the compactness does not depend on
\varepsilon
or
\mu
.

See Appendix
C
for a proof.

Remark 3.13

(compact embeddings)
.

The embeddings

D(\A_{0})\cap D(\A_{-1}^{*})=D(\A_{0})=\sfH^{2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow\sfL^{2}(\Omega),\quad D(\A_{3})\cap D(\A_{2}^{*})=D(\A_{2}^{*})=\sfH^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow\sfL^{2}(\Omega)

are compact by Rellich’s selection theorem.

Theorem 3.14

(compact biharmonic complex)
.

The long primal and dual biharmonic Hilbert complex (
8
) is compact.
In particular, the complex is closed.

Let us recall for the densely defined and closed linear operators

\A_{n}:D(\A_{n})\subset\sfH_{n}\to\sfH_{n+1},\qquad\A_{n}^{*}:D(\A_{n}^{*})\subset\sfH_{n+1}\to\sfH_{n}

the corresponding reduced operators

\displaystyle(\A_{n})_{\bot}:=\A_{n}\big|_{N(\A_{n})^{\bot_{\sfH_{n}}}}:D\big((\A_{n})_{\bot}\big)=D(\A_{n})\cap N(\A_{n})^{\bot_{\sfH_{n}}}\subset N(\A_{n})^{\bot_{\sfH_{n}}}

\displaystyle\to R(\A_{n}),

\displaystyle(\A_{n}^{*})_{\bot}:=\A_{n}^{*}\big|_{N(\A_{n}^{*})^{\bot_{\sfH_{n+1}}}}:D\big((\A_{n}^{*})_{\bot}\big)=D(\A_{n}^{*})\cap N(\A_{n}^{*})^{\bot_{\sfH_{n+1}}}\subset N(\A_{n}^{*})^{\bot_{\sfH_{n+1}}}

\displaystyle\to R(\A_{n}^{*}).

Note that
R(\A_{n})=R\big((\A_{n})_{\bot}\big)=N(\A_{n}^{*})^{\bot_{\sfH_{n+1}}}

and
R(\A_{n}^{*})=R\big((\A_{n}^{*})_{\bot}\big)=N(\A_{n})^{\bot_{\sfH_{n}}}
.
Here, we consider

\displaystyle(\A_{0})_{\bot}

\displaystyle=(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})_{\bot},

\displaystyle(\A_{1})_{\bot}

\displaystyle=(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})_{\bot},

\displaystyle(\A_{2})_{\bot}

\displaystyle=(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}\mu)_{\bot},

\displaystyle(\A_{0}^{*})_{\bot}

\displaystyle=(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)_{\bot},

\displaystyle(\A_{1}^{*})_{\bot}

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}})_{\bot},

\displaystyle(\A_{2}^{*})_{\bot}

\displaystyle=-(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})_{\bot},

and

\displaystyle(\A_{-1})_{\bot}=(\iota_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}})_{\bot}=\id_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}:\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}},

\displaystyle(\A_{-1}^{*})_{\bot}\cong(\pi_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}})_{\bot}=\pi_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}\big|_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}=\id_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}}:\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}},

\displaystyle(\A_{3})_{\bot}\cong(\pi_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}})_{\bot}=\pi_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}\big|_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}=\id_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}:\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}

\displaystyle\to\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}},

\displaystyle(\A_{3}^{*})_{\bot}=(\iota_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}})_{\bot}=\id_{\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}}:\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}

\displaystyle\to\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}}.

[
3
, Lemma 2.9]
shows:

Theorem 3.15

(mini FA-ToolBox)
.

For the zero order biharmonic complex it holds:

(i)

The ranges
R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})
,
R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})
, and
R(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}\mu)

are closed.

(i’)

The ranges
R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)
,
R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}})
, and
R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})

are closed.

(ii)

The inverse operators
(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})_{\bot}^{-1}
,
(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})_{\bot}^{-1}
,
and
(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}\mu)_{\bot}^{-1}
are compact.

(ii’)

The inverse operators
(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)_{\bot}^{-1}
,
(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}})_{\bot}^{-1}
,
and
(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})_{\bot}^{-1}
are compact.

(iii)

The cohomology groups of generalised Dirichlet/Neumann tensor fields

\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)
and
\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)

Are finite-dimensional.
Moreover, the dimensions do not depend on
\varepsilon
or
\mu
.

(iv)

The orthonormal Helmholtz type decompositions

\displaystyle\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=N(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}})

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}),

\displaystyle\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}\mu)

\displaystyle=N(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})

hold.

(v)

There exist (optimal)
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that
the Friedrichs/Poincaré type estimates

\displaystyle\forall\,u

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle|u|_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}|\Gradgrad u|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\forall\,S

\displaystyle\in\varepsilon^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})

\displaystyle|S|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}|\divDiv\varepsilon S|_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\forall\,S

\displaystyle\in\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}})

\displaystyle|S|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}|\mu^{-1}\Rot S|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\forall\,T

\displaystyle\in\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\symRot,\Omega)\cap R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})

\displaystyle|T|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}|\varepsilon^{-1}\symRot T|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\forall\,T

\displaystyle\in\mu^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})

\displaystyle|T|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}|\Div\mu T|_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\forall\,v

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\cap(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle|v|_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}|\devGrad v|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}

hold.

(vi)

For all

S\in\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\varepsilon^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}}

it holds

|S|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}^{2}\leq c_{1}^{2}|\mu^{-1}\Rot S|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}^{2}+c_{0}^{2}|\divDiv\varepsilon S|_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

(vi’)

For all

T\in\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\symRot,\Omega)\cap\mu^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}}

it holds

|T|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}^{2}\leq c_{1}^{2}|\varepsilon^{-1}\symRot T|_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}^{2}+c_{2}^{2}|\Div\mu T|_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

(vii)

\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)=\{0\}

and
\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)=\{0\}
,
if
(\Omega,\Gamma_{\!t})
is extendable.

Theorem 3.16

(regular representations and closed ranges)
.

Let
k\geq 0
. Then the regular potential representations

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\Gradgrad\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)=\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\Rot\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)=\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle R(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\Div\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)=\Div\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap R(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\devGrad\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\devGrad,\Omega)=\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\symRot\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)=\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\varepsilon}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\varepsilon}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})=R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\divDiv\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)=\divDiv\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\divDiv\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

hold. In particular, the latter spaces are closed subspaces of

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)
,
\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)
, and
\sfH^{k}(\Omega)
, respectively,
and all ranges of the higher Sobolev order operators
in (
9
) are closed.
Moroever, the long biharmonic Hilbert complexes (
10
)–(
13
)
are closed.

A proof is given in Appendix
C
.
Note that in Theorem
3.16

we claim nothing about bounded regular potential operators,
leaving the question of bounded potentials to the next sections,
cf. Theorem
3.24
.

The reduced operators corresponding to (
9
) are

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}:D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)\subset(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1})_{\bot}:D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1})_{\bot}\big)\subset(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k-1}),\quad k\geq 1,

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}:D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)\subset N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}:D\big((\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)\subset N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to R(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})_{\bot}:D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)\subset(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}),

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})_{\bot}:D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)\subset N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}),

\displaystyle(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})_{\bot}:D\big((\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)\subset N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}),

\displaystyle(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1})_{\bot}:D\big((\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,k-1})_{\bot}\big)\subset N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k-1}),\quad k\geq 1.

[
3
, Lemma 2.1]
and Theorem
3.16
yield:

Theorem 3.17

(closed ranges and bounded inverse operators)
.

Let
k\geq 0
. Then:

(i)

R\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})=R\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}\big)

are closed and, equivalently, the inverse operators

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)

\displaystyle\text{resp.}\qquad(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}\big)

\displaystyle\text{resp.}\qquad(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})

are bounded. Equivalently, there is
c>0
such that for all

u\in D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)
resp.
u\in D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}\big)

|u|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)}\leq c|\Gradgrad u|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}\quad\text{resp.}\quad|u|_{\sfH^{k+1}(\Omega)}\leq c|\Gradgrad u|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}.

(ii)

R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=R\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)
are closed and, equivalently,
the inverse operator

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)

\displaystyle\text{resp.}\qquad(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

is bounded. Equivalently, there is
c>0
such that
for all
S\in D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)

|S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}\leq c|\Rot S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}.

(iii)

R(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=R\big((\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)
are closed and, equivalently,
the inverse operator

\displaystyle(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D\big((\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)

\displaystyle\text{resp.}\qquad(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

is bounded. Equivalently, there is
c>0
such that
for all
T\in D\big((\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)

|T|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}\leq c|\Div T|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)}.

(iv)

R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=R\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)
are closed and, equivalently,
the inverse operator

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)

\displaystyle\text{resp.}\qquad(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

is bounded. Equivalently, there is
c>0
such that
for all
v\in D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)

|v|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)}\leq c|\devGrad v|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}.

(v)

R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=R\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)
are closed and, equivalently,
the inverse operator

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)

\displaystyle\text{resp.}\qquad(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

is bounded. Equivalently, there is
c>0
such that
for all
T\in D\big((\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)

|T|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}\leq c|\symRot T|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}.

(vi)

R\big((\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)=R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})=R\big((\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}\big)

are closed and, equivalently, the inverse operators

\displaystyle(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D\big((\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)

\displaystyle\text{resp.}\qquad(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D\big((\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}\big)

\displaystyle\text{resp.}\qquad(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})

are bounded. Equivalently, there is
c>0
such that for all

S\in D\big((\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}\big)
resp.
S\in D\big((\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}\big)

|S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}\leq c|\divDiv S|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)}\quad\text{resp.}\quad|S|_{\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}\leq c|\divDiv S|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)}.

Lemma 3.18

(Schwarz’ lemma)
.

Let
0\leq|\alpha|\leq k
.

(i)

If
S\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)
then

\p^{\alpha}S\in\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

and
\Rot\p^{\alpha}S=\p^{\alpha}\Rot S
.

(ii)

If
T\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)
then

\p^{\alpha}T\in\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)
and
\Div\p^{\alpha}T=\p^{\alpha}\Div T
.

(iii)

If
T\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)
then

\p^{\alpha}T\in\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)
and
\symRot\p^{\alpha}T=\p^{\alpha}\symRot T
.

(iv)

If
S\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
resp.

S\in\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
then

\p^{\alpha}S\in\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
resp.

\p^{\alpha}S\in\sfH^{1,0}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

and
\divDiv\p^{\alpha}S=\p^{\alpha}\divDiv S
.

Theorem 3.19

(compact embedding)
.

Let
k\geq 0
. Then the embeddings

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma}(\Omega)

are compact.

A proof is given in Appendix
C
.

Remark 3.20

(compact embedding)
.

For
k\geq 1
, cf.
[
7
, Remark 4.12]
, there is
another and slightly more general proof of the first compact embedding
using a variant of
[
3
, Lemma 2.22]
,
cf.
[
4
, Theorem 3.19, Remark 3.20]
,
see Appendix
C
for a proof.
It utilises the decomposition

\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

from Lemma
3.10

and leads immediately to the next (stronger) result.

Theorem 3.21

(compact embedding)
.

Let
k\geq 1
. Then the embedding

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)\hookrightarrow\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma}(\Omega)

is compact.

Theorem 3.22

(Friedrichs/Poincaré type estimate)
.

Let
k\geq 0
.
Then there exists
c>0
such that for all

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}

it holds

\displaystyle|S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c\big(|\Rot S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}+|\divDiv S|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)}\big),

\displaystyle|T|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c\big(|\symRot T|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}+|\Div T|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)}\big)

respectively. The orthogonality condition
\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}}

and
\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}

can be replaced by the weaker conditions

\mcH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}}
or

\mcH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}}

and
\mcH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}
or

\mcH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}
,
respectively. In particular,

\displaystyle\forall\,S

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})

\displaystyle|S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c|\Rot S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\forall\,S

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle|S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c|\divDiv S|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\forall\,T

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})

\displaystyle|T|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c|\symRot T|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\forall\,T

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Div,\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle|T|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c|\Div T|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)}

with

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}},

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}.

Analogously, for
k\geq 1
there exists
c>0
such that

|S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}\leq c\big(|\Rot S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}+|\divDiv S|_{\sfH^{k-1}(\Omega)}\big)

for all
S
in
\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\id}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}}
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\forall\,S

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle|S|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq c|\divDiv S|_{\sfH^{k-1}(\Omega)}.

The proof follows by a standard contradiction argument.

Remark 3.23

(Friedrichs/Poincaré/Korn type estimate)
.

Let
k\geq 0
.
Similar to Theorem
3.22

and by Rellich’s selection theorem, cf. the estimates in Theorem
3.17
,
there exists
c>0
such that for all

v\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

and for all
u\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

|v|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)}\leq c|\devGrad v|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)},\qquad|u|_{\sfH^{k}(\Omega)}\leq|u|_{\sfH^{k+1}(\Omega)}\leq c|\Gradgrad u|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}.

As in Theorem
3.17
,

(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}
and
(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}
can be replaced
by
(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!t}})^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)}}
and
(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})^{\bot_{\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)}}
, respectively.

3.3.
Regular Potentials and Decompositions II

Let
k\geq 0
.
According to Theorem
3.17
the inverses
of the reduced operators

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})=\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega),

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}^{-1}:R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\to D(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})=\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

are bounded and we recall the bounded linear regular decomposition operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k,1}:\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k,0}:\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

from Lemma
3.10
.
Similar to
[
3
, Theorem 4.18, Theorem 5.2]

and
[
4
, Theorem 3.24, Theorem 3.25]
,
cf.
[
3
, Lemma 2.22, Theorem 2.23]
,
we obtain the following sequence of results:

Theorem 3.24

(bounded regular potentials from bounded regular decompositions)
.

For
k\geq 0
there exist bounded linear regular potential operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:=(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}:=(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}^{-1}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:=(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\varepsilon}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})_{\bot}^{-1}:\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k,1}(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})_{\bot}^{-1}:\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

such that

\displaystyle\Gradgrad\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}=\Gradgrad\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
