\displaystyle=\id|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}}},

\displaystyle\Rot\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}},

\displaystyle\Div\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id|_{\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\RT_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}},

\displaystyle\devGrad\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}}},

\displaystyle\symRot\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\varepsilon}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}}},

\displaystyle\divDiv\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}=\divDiv\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}

\displaystyle=\id|_{\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}}.

In particular, all potentials in Theorem
3.16

can be chosen such that they depend continuously on the data.

\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,

\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}

are right inverses of
\Gradgrad
,
\Rot
,
\Div
,
\devGrad
,
\symRot
,
\divDiv
, respectively.

Theorem 3.25

(bounded regular decompositions from bounded regular potentials)
.

For
k\geq 0
the bounded regular decompositions

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus R(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus R(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus R(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus R(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k,1})\dotplus R(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})

hold with bounded linear regular decomposition operators

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k,1}:=\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}:\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}:\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}:\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

satisfying

\displaystyle\id_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k},

\displaystyle\id_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k},

\displaystyle\id_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k},

\displaystyle\id_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k},

\displaystyle\id_{\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k,1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k}.

Corollary 3.26

(bounded regular kernel decompositions)
.

For
k\geq 0
the bounded regular kernel decompositions

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)+\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)+\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)+\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

hold.

As in
[
4
, Remark 3.27, Theorem 3.28]

and
[
3
, Theorem 4.18, Remark 4.19, Theorem 5.2, Remark 5.3]
,
cf.
[
3
, Sections 2.3 and 2.4]
,
there is a collection of results about the
bounded regular decomposition operators,
see Remark
D.1
and Remark
D.2

of Appendix
D
.

Corollary
3.26
shows:

Corollary 3.27

(bounded regular higher order kernel decompositions)
.

For
k,\ell\geq 0
the bounded regular kernel decompositions

\displaystyle N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)+\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)+\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)+\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

hold. In particular, for
k=0
and all
\ell\geq 0

\displaystyle N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)+\Gradgrad\sfH^{2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)+\Rot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)+\devGrad\sfH^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega).

3.4.
Dirichlet/Neumann Fields

From Theorem
3.15
(iv) we recall the
slightly modified orthonormal Helmholtz type decompositions

(14)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)\\
&=N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}})\\
&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}),\\
N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega),\\
N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)&=\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}),\\
\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)\\
&=N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}})\\
&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}),\\
N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega),\\
N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)&=\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}).\end{aligned}

Let us denote the
\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)
- and
\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)
-orthonormal projectors onto

N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)
,
N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})

and
N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)
,
N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})
by

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}:\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)}:\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu),

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})}:\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}),

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})}:\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}),

respectively. Then

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}|_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})}:N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})

\displaystyle\to\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})}|_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}:N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\to\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)}|_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})}:N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})

\displaystyle\to\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})}|_{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)}:N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)

\displaystyle\to\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)

are onto. Moreover,

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}|_{R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)}|_{R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})}|_{R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}})}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})}|_{R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}})}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}|_{\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)}|_{\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})}|_{\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})}|_{\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\id_{\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)}.

Therefore, by Corollary
3.27
and for all
\ell\geq 0

\displaystyle\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})}N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)=\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})}\varepsilon^{-1}\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\pi_{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)}N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\pi_{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)}\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})}N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)=\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})}\mu^{-1}\sfH^{\ell}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega),

where we have used

N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)=\varepsilon^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),\qquad N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)=\mu^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega).

Hence with

\displaystyle\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{k\geq 0}\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{k\geq 0}\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{k\geq 0}\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{k\geq 0}\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega),

and with the finite numbers

d_{\Omega,\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}:=\dim\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega),\qquad d_{\Omega,\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}:=\dim\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)

we get the following result:

Theorem 3.28

(smooth pre-bases of Dirichlet/Neumann fields)
.

It holds

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)=\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})}\varepsilon^{-1}\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\pi_{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)}\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)=\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})}\mu^{-1}\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega).

Moreover, there exist smooth
\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}
and
\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}

pre-bases

of
\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)

and smooth
\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}
and
\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}

pre-bases

of
\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)
, i.e.,
there are linear independent smooth fields

\displaystyle\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle:=\{B^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{d_{\Omega,\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}\subset\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle:=\{B^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{d_{\Omega,\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}\subset\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle:=\{B^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{d_{\Omega,\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}\subset\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle:=\{B^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{d_{\Omega,\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}\subset\sfH^{\infty}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega),

such that
\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

and
\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})}\varepsilon^{-1}\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)

are both bases of
\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)
,
and
\pi_{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)}\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

and
\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})}\mu^{-1}\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)

are both bases of
\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)
.
In particular,

\displaystyle\Lin\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)=\Lin\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})}\varepsilon^{-1}\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\Lin\pi_{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\mu)}\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)=\Lin\pi_{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})}\mu^{-1}\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega).

Note that, e.g.,
(1-\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)})
is the
\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)
-orthonormal projector
onto
R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})
.
By (
14
), Theorem
3.16
,
and Theorem
3.28
we compute

(15)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)\\
&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}\Lin\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)\\
&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})+(\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}-1)\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)+\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)\\
&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})+\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega),\\
\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})\cap\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)+\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega),\\
&=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})+\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega).\end{aligned}

Similarly, we obtain decompositions of

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)
,
\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)
,
and
\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega)

using
\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)
,
\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)
, and
\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)
,
respectively. We conclude:

Theorem 3.29

(bounded regular direct decompositions)
.

Let
k\geq 0
.
Then the bounded regular direct decompositions

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k+1,k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)

hold. Note that

R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\subset\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
,

R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,1})\subset\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)
,

R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\subset\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
,
and
R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,1}),R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k+1,k,1})\subset\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)
.

See Appendix
C
for a proof.

Remark 3.30

(bounded regular direct decompositions)
.

In particular, for
k=0

\displaystyle\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{0,1})\dotplus\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Gradgrad\sfH^{2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Gradgrad\sfH^{2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{0,1})\dotplus\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega),

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}\sfH^{0}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\mu^{-1}\Rot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\dotplus\mu^{-1}\Lin\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\mu^{-1}\Rot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{0,1})\dotplus\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\devGrad\sfH^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\devGrad\sfH^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{0,1})\dotplus\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-1}\symRot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\dotplus\varepsilon^{-1}\Lin\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-1}\symRot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega),

and

\displaystyle\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}\varepsilon^{-1}\symRot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Gradgrad\sfH^{2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}\varepsilon^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\divDiv,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}\mu^{-1}\Rot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\devGrad\sfH^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\oplus_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}\mu^{-1}\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},0}(\Div,\Omega).

By the latter theorem we have bounded linear regular (direct) decompositions

(16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)&=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)\dotplus\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\\
&=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),\\
\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Div,\Omega)&=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,1})\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)\dotplus\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\\
&=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)+\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega),\\
\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)&=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)\dotplus\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\\
&=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),\\
\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)&=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k,1})\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)\dotplus\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\\
&=\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega),\\
\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)&=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k+1,k,1})\dotplus\Lin\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)\dotplus\symRot\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)\\
&=\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega),\end{aligned}

see Remark
D.3
for more details
on these decompositions and the corresponding
bounded linear regular direct decomposition operators.
Noting

(17)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}})&\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega),&R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})&\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega),\\
R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}})&\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega),&R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}})&\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)\end{aligned}

we see:

Theorem 3.31

(alternative Dirichlet/Neumann projections)
.

It holds

\displaystyle\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)\cap\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=\{0\},

\displaystyle N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)\cap\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}),

\displaystyle\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)\cap\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=\{0\},

\displaystyle N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})\cap\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}),

\displaystyle\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)\cap\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=\{0\},

\displaystyle N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)\cap\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}),

\displaystyle\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)\cap\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=\{0\},

\displaystyle N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}})\cap\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}).

Moreover, for all
k\geq 0

\displaystyle N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}\varepsilon)\cap\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\varepsilon}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=R(\varepsilon^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})=\varepsilon^{-1}\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\cap\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k}\varepsilon)\cap\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T},\mu}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=R(\mu^{-1}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})=\mu^{-1}\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\cap\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega).

See Appendix
C
for a proof.
Theorem
3.29
implies:

Theorem 3.32

(cohomology groups)
.

It holds

\displaystyle\frac{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})}{R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})}\cong\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\cong\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)\cong\Lin\mcB^{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)\cong\frac{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})}{R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})},

\displaystyle\frac{N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})}{R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})}\cong\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\cong\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\mu}(\Omega)\cong\Lin\mcB^{\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}}(\Omega)\cong\frac{N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})}{R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})}.

In particular, the dimensions of the cohomology groups
(Dirichlet/Neumann fields) are independent of
k
and
\varepsilon
,
\mu
and it holds

\displaystyle d_{\Omega,\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}

\displaystyle=\dim\big(N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})/R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\big)=\dim\big(N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})/R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})\big),

\displaystyle d_{\Omega,\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}

\displaystyle=\dim\big(N(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})/R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})\big)=\dim\big(N(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})/R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}^{k})\big).

Appendix A
Elementary Formulas

From
[
6
,
7
]
and
[
5
]
we
have the following collection of formulas related to the elasticity and
the biharmonic complex.

Lemma A.1

(
[
5
, Lemma 12.10]
)
.

Let
u
,
v
,
w
, and
S
belong to
\sfC^{\infty}(\reals^{3})
.

•

(\spn v)\,w=v\times w=-(\spn w)\,v

and

(\spn v)(\spn^{-1}S)=-Sv
, if
\sym S=0

•

\sym\spn v=0

and
\dev(u\id)=0

•

\tr\Grad v=\divergence v

and
2\skw\Grad v=\spn\rot v

•

\Div(u\id)=\grad u

and
\Rot(u\id)=-\spn\grad u
,

in particular,
\rot\Div(u\id)=0

and
\rot\spn^{-1}\Rot(u\id)=0

and
\sym\Rot(u\id)=0

•

\Div\spn v=-\rot v

and
\Div\skw S=-\rot\spn^{-1}\skw S
,

in particular,
\divergence\Div\skw S=0

•

\Rot\spn v=(\divergence v)\id-(\Grad v)^{\top}

and
\Rot\skw S=(\divergence\spn^{-1}\skw S)\id-(\Grad\spn^{-1}\skw S)^{\top}

•

\dev\Rot\spn v=-(\dev\Grad v)^{\top}

•

-2\Rot\sym\Grad v=2\Rot\skw\Grad v=-(\Grad\rot v)^{\top}

•

2\spn^{-1}\skw\Rot S=\Div S^{\top}-\grad\tr S=\Div\big(S-(\tr S)\id\big)^{\top}
,

in particular,
\rot\Div S^{\top}=2\rot\spn^{-1}\skw\Rot S

and
2\skw\Rot S=\spn\Div S^{\top}
, if
\tr S=0

•

\tr\Rot S=2\divergence\spn^{-1}\skw S
,
in particular,
\tr\Rot S=0
, if
\skw S=0
,

and
\tr\Rot\sym S=0
and
\tr\Rot\skw S=\tr\Rot S

•

2(\Grad\spn^{-1}\skw S)^{\top}=(\tr\Rot\skw S)\id-2\Rot\skw S

•

3\Div(\dev\Grad v)^{\top}=2\grad\divergence v

•

2\Rot\sym\Grad v=-2\Rot\skw\Grad v=-\Rot\spn\rot v=(\Grad\rot v)^{\top}

•

2\Div\sym\Rot S=-2\Div\skw\Rot S=\rot\Div S^{\top}

•

\Rot(\Rot\sym S)^{\top}=\sym\Rot(\Rot S)^{\top}

•

\Rot(\Rot\skw S)^{\top}=\skw\Rot(\Rot S)^{\top}

All formulas extend also to distributions.

Appendix B
Biharmonic Complex Operators Revisited

Let
\top
denote the formal operator of matrix transposition, i.e.,

\top S:=S^{\top},

and define

\spn:\reals^{3}\to\reals^{3\times 3}_{\skw};\,\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}\\
a_{2}\\
a_{3}\end{bmatrix}\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}0&-a_{3}&a_{2}\\
a_{3}&0&-a_{1}\\
-a_{2}&a_{1}&0\end{bmatrix}.

We recall the operators forming the de Rham complex (classical vector analysis)

\grad
,
\rot
, and
\divergence
acting on functions and vector fields, respectively,
as formal matrix operators

\displaystyle\grad

\displaystyle:=\begin{bmatrix}\p_{1}\\
\p_{2}\\
\p_{3}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\rot

\displaystyle:=\spn\grad=\begin{bmatrix}0&-\p_{3}&\p_{2}\\
\p_{3}&0&-\p_{1}\\
-\p_{2}&\p_{1}&0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\divergence

\displaystyle:=\top\grad=\begin{bmatrix}\p_{1}&\p_{2}&\p_{3}\end{bmatrix}.

Moreover, we introduce their relatives from the vector de Rham complex
acting on vector and tensor fields, respectively, as formal matrix operators

\displaystyle\Grad

\displaystyle:=\top\grad\top,

\displaystyle\Rot

\displaystyle:=\top\rot\top,

\displaystyle\Div

\displaystyle:=\top\divergence\top.

In words,
\Grad
,
\Rot
, and
\Div
act row-wise as the operators

\grad
,
\rot
, and
\divergence
from the classical de Rham complex.
Note that
\Grad v
is just the Jacobian for a vector field
v
.

Let

\iota_{\mathbb{S}}:\reals^{3\times 3}_{\sym}\to\reals^{3\times 3},\qquad\iota_{\mathbb{T}}:\reals^{3\times 3}_{\dev}\to\reals^{3\times 3}

denote the canonical embedding of symmetric and deviatoric (trace free)
(3\times 3)
-matrices
into the arbitrary
(3\times 3)
-matrices, respectively.
Then the adjoints

\iota_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}:\reals^{3\times 3}\to\reals^{3\times 3}_{\sym},\qquad\iota_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}:\reals^{3\times 3}\to\reals^{3\times 3}_{\dev}

are almost the projectors onto symmetric and deviatoric
(3\times 3)
-matrices, respectively, i.e.,
the actual projectors are given by

\sym:=\iota_{\mathbb{S}}\iota_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}:\reals^{3\times 3}\to\reals^{3\times 3};\,S\mapsto\frac{1}{2}(S+S^{\top}),\quad\dev:=\iota_{\mathbb{T}}\iota_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}:\reals^{3\times 3}\to\reals^{3\times 3};\,T\mapsto T-\frac{1}{3}(\tr T)\id.

We extend all the latter formal operators to
\sfL^{2}(\Omega)
-tensor fields.

In the light of this, in the biharmonic complexes we are dealing with the operators

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}

\displaystyle:=\iota_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\Grad\grad,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}}

\displaystyle:=\iota_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}\Rot\iota_{\mathbb{S}},

\displaystyle\Div_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle:=\Div\iota_{\mathbb{T}},

\displaystyle\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}}

\displaystyle:=\divergence\Div\iota_{\mathbb{S}},

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle:=\iota_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\Rot\iota_{\mathbb{T}},

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}

\displaystyle:=\iota_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}\Grad.

Note that

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathbb{S}}\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}

\displaystyle=\sym\Gradgrad=\Gradgrad=\top\grad\top\grad,

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathbb{T}}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}}

\displaystyle=\dev\Rot_{\mathbb{S}}=\Rot\iota_{\mathbb{S}}=\top\rot\top\iota_{\mathbb{S}}=:\Rot_{\mathbb{S}},

\displaystyle\Div_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle=\top\divergence\top\iota_{\mathbb{T}},

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathbb{T}}\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}

\displaystyle=\devGrad=\dev\top\grad\top,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle=\symRot_{\mathbb{T}}=\sym\top\rot\top\iota_{\mathbb{T}},

\displaystyle\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}}

\displaystyle=\divergence\top\divergence\top\iota_{\mathbb{S}},

in particular, on symmetric tensor fields we have

\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}}=\devRot=\Rot
, cf.
[
7
, Lemma A.1]
.
Using these formal operators we introduce
their maximal
\sfL^{2}(\Omega)
-realisations, i.e.,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad})\subset\sfL^{2}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto\Gradgrad u,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}})\subset\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto\Rot S,

\displaystyle\Div_{\mathbb{T}}:D(\Div_{\mathbb{T}})\subset\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfL^{2}(\Omega),

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\Div T,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad})\subset\sfL^{2}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\mapsto\devGrad v,

\displaystyle\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}}:D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}})\subset\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega),

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto\symRot T,

\displaystyle\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}}:D(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}})\subset\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\to\sfL^{2}(\Omega),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto\divDiv S,

which are densely defined and closed (unbounded) linear operators
and form the two (formally primal and dual) biharmonic complexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Div_{\mathbb{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

cf.
[
6
]
for the complex properties.

Finally, the operators

\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}},\quad\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}},\quad\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}},\quad\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}},\quad\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}},\quad\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}

from Section
2.1
are the restrictions of

\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\quad\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\quad\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\quad\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad},\quad\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\quad\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}}

to their domains of defintion

D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}),\quad D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}),\quad D(\Div_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}),\quad D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}),\quad D(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}),\quad D(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})

which are the closures of
\sfC^{\infty}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
,
\sfC^{\infty}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
,
and
\sfC^{\infty}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
in the corresponding graph norms, respectively.

Appendix C
Some Proofs

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

In
[
6
, Theorem 3.10]
we have shown the stated results for
\Gamma_{\!t}=\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{\!t}=\emptyset
,
which is also a crucial ingredient of this proof.
Note that in these two special cases always “
strong
=
weak
” holds
as
\A_{n}^{**}=\overline{\A_{n}}=\A_{n}
, and that this argument fails in the remaining cases
of mixed boundary conditions. Therefore, let
\emptyset\varsubsetneq\Gamma_{\!t}\varsubsetneq\Gamma
.
Moreover, recall the notion of an extendable domain from
[
3
, Section 3]
.
In particular,
\widehat{\Omega}
and the extended domain

\widetilde{\Omega}
are topologically trivial.

•

Let
S\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)
.
By definition,
S
can be extended through
\Gamma_{\!t}

by zero to the larger domain
\widetilde{\Omega}
yielding

\widetilde{S}\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\emptyset,0}(\Rot,\widetilde{\Omega})=\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},0}(\Rot,\widetilde{\Omega})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},0}(\Rot,\widetilde{\Omega}).

By
[
6
, Theorem 3.10, Remark 3.11]

and Stein’s or Calderon’s extension theorem
– see also
[
3
, Lemma 4.3, Lemma 4.4]
for the fact that
the respective potentials are already defined on the whole of
\reals^{3}
–
there exists
\widetilde{u}\in\sfH^{k+2}(\reals^{3})

such that
\Gradgrad\widetilde{u}=\widetilde{S}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
.
Since
\widetilde{S}=0
in
\widehat{\Omega}
,
\widetilde{u}
must be a polynomial

p\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
in
\widehat{\Omega}
. Far outside of
\widetilde{\Omega}
we modify
p

by a cut-off function such that the resulting function
\widetilde{p}

is compactly supported and
\widetilde{p}|_{\widetilde{\Omega}}=p
.
Note that
\widetilde{p}
depends continuously on
S

by Poincaré’s estimate.
Then
u:=\widetilde{u}-\widetilde{p}\in\sfH^{k+2}(\reals^{3})

with
u|_{\widehat{\Omega}}=0
. Hence
u
belongs to
\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

and depends continuously on
S
. Moreover,
u
satisfies

\Gradgrad u=\Gradgrad\widetilde{u}=\widetilde{S}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
,
in particular
\Gradgrad u=S
in
\Omega
.
We put
\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}S:=u\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.

•

Let
T\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)
. By definition,
T
can be extended through
\Gamma_{\!t}

by zero to
\widetilde{\Omega}
giving

\widetilde{T}\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\emptyset,0}(\Div,\widetilde{\Omega})=\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},0}(\Div,\widetilde{\Omega})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},0}(\Div,\widetilde{\Omega}).

By
[
6
, Theorem 3.10]
there exists
\widetilde{S}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S}}(\reals^{3})

such that
\Rot\widetilde{S}=\widetilde{T}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
.
Since
\widetilde{T}=0
in
\widehat{\Omega}
, i.e.,

\widetilde{S}|_{\widehat{\Omega}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},0}(\Rot,\widehat{\Omega})
,
we get again by
[
6
, Theorem 3.10]

(or the first part of this proof)
\widetilde{u}\in\sfH^{k+3}(\reals^{3})

such that
\Gradgrad\widetilde{u}=\widetilde{S}
in
\widehat{\Omega}
.
Then
S:=\widetilde{S}-\Gradgrad\widetilde{u}

belongs to
\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S}}(\reals^{3})
and satisfies
S|_{\widehat{\Omega}}=0
.
Thus
S\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and depends continuously on
T
.
Furthermore,
\Rot S=\Rot\widetilde{S}=\widetilde{T}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
,
in particular
\Rot S=T
in
\Omega
.
We set
\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}T:=S\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.

•

Let
v\in\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
. By definition,
v
can be extended through
\Gamma_{\!t}

by zero to
\widetilde{\Omega}
defining
\widetilde{v}\in\sfH^{k}(\widetilde{\Omega})
.

[
6
, Theorem 3.10]
yields
\widetilde{T}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T}}(\reals^{3})

such that
\Div\widetilde{T}=\widetilde{v}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
.
As
\widetilde{v}=0
in
\widehat{\Omega}
, i.e.,

\widetilde{T}|_{\widehat{\Omega}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},0}(\Div,\widehat{\Omega})
,
we get again by
[
6
, Theorem 3.10]

(or the second part of this proof)
\widetilde{S}\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\reals^{3})

such that
\Rot\widetilde{S}=\widetilde{T}
holds in
\widehat{\Omega}
.
Then
T:=\widetilde{T}-\Rot\widetilde{S}

belongs to
\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T}}(\reals^{3})
with
T|_{\widehat{\Omega}}=0
.
Hence
T
belongs to
\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and depends continuously on
v
.
Furthermore,
\Div T=\Div\widetilde{T}=\widetilde{v}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
,
in particular
\Div T=v
in
\Omega
.
Finally, we define
\mathcal{P}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}v:=T\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.

•

Let
T\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\symRot,\Omega)
.
By definition,
T
can be extended through
\Gamma_{\!t}

by zero to
\widetilde{\Omega}
yielding

\widetilde{T}\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\emptyset,0}(\symRot,\widetilde{\Omega})=\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},0}(\symRot,\widetilde{\Omega})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},0}(\symRot,\widetilde{\Omega}).

By
[
6
, Theorem 3.10]
there exists
\widetilde{v}\in\sfH^{k+1}(\reals^{3})

such that
\devGrad\widetilde{v}=\widetilde{T}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
.
Since
\widetilde{T}=0
in
\widehat{\Omega}
,
\widetilde{v}
must be a Raviart-Thomas field

r\in\RT
in
\widehat{\Omega}
. Far outside of
\widetilde{\Omega}
we modify
r

by a cut-off function such that the resulting vector field
\widetilde{r}

is compactly supported and
\widetilde{r}|_{\widetilde{\Omega}}=r
.
Then
v:=\widetilde{v}-\widetilde{r}\in\sfH^{k+1}(\reals^{3})

with
v|_{\widehat{\Omega}}=0
. Hence
v
belongs to
\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

and depends continuously on
T
. Moreover,
v
satisfies

\devGrad v=\devGrad\widetilde{v}=\widetilde{T}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
,
in particular
\devGrad v=T
in
\Omega
.
We put
\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Grad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}T:=v\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.

•

Let
S\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\divDiv,\Omega)
. By definition,
S
can be extended through
\Gamma_{\!t}

by zero to
\widetilde{\Omega}
giving

\widetilde{S}\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\emptyset,0}(\divDiv,\widetilde{\Omega})=\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},0}(\divDiv,\widetilde{\Omega})=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},0}(\divDiv,\widetilde{\Omega}).

By
[
6
, Theorem 3.10]
there exists
\widetilde{T}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T}}(\reals^{3})

such that
\symRot\widetilde{T}=\widetilde{S}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
.
Since
\widetilde{S}=0
in
\widehat{\Omega}
, i.e.,

\widetilde{T}|_{\widehat{\Omega}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},0}(\symRot,\widehat{\Omega})
,
we get again by
[
6
, Theorem 3.10]

(or the fourth part of this proof)
\widetilde{v}\in\sfH^{k+2}(\reals^{3})

such that
\devGrad\widetilde{v}=\widetilde{T}
in
\widehat{\Omega}
.
Then
T:=\widetilde{T}-\devGrad\widetilde{v}

belongs to
\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T}}(\reals^{3})
and satisfies
T|_{\widehat{\Omega}}=0
.
Thus
T\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and depends continuously on
S
.
Furthermore,
\symRot T=\symRot\widetilde{T}=\widetilde{S}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
,
in particular
\symRot T=S
in
\Omega
.
We set
\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}S:=T\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.

•

Let
u\in\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
. By definition,
u
can be extended through
\Gamma_{\!t}

by zero to
\widetilde{\Omega}
defining
\widetilde{u}\in\sfH^{k}(\widetilde{\Omega})
.

[
6
, Theorem 3.10]
yields
\widetilde{S}\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\reals^{3})

such that
\divDiv\widetilde{S}=\widetilde{u}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
.
As
\widetilde{u}=0
in
\widehat{\Omega}
, i.e.,

\widetilde{S}|_{\widehat{\Omega}}\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},0}(\divDiv,\widehat{\Omega})
,
we get again by
[
6
, Theorem 3.10]

(or the fifth part of this proof)
\widetilde{T}\in\sfH^{k+3}_{\mathbb{T}}(\reals^{3})

such that
\symRot\widetilde{T}=\widetilde{S}
holds in
\widehat{\Omega}
.
Then
S:=\widetilde{S}-\symRot\widetilde{T}

belongs to
\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\reals^{3})
with
S|_{\widehat{\Omega}}=0
.
Hence
S
belongs to
\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and depends continuously on
u
.
Furthermore,
\divDiv S=\divDiv\widetilde{S}=\widetilde{u}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
,
in particular
\divDiv S=u
in
\Omega
.
Finally, we define
\mathcal{P}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}u:=S\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.

The assertion about the compact supports is trivial.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.10
and Corollary
3.11
.

According to
[
1
, Section 4.2]
,
cf.
[
2
, Section 4.2]
,
[
3
, Lemma 3.1]
,
[
4
]
, or
[
7
]
,
let
(U_{\ell},\varphi_{\ell})
be a partition of unity for
\Omega
, such that

\Omega=\bigcup_{\ell=-L}^{L}\Omega_{\ell},\qquad\Omega_{\ell}:=\Omega\cap U_{\ell},\qquad\varphi_{\ell}\in\sfC^{\infty}_{\p U_{\ell}}(U_{\ell}),

and such that
(\Omega_{\ell},\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell})
are extendable bounded strong Lipschitz pairs.
Recall

\Sigma_{\ell}:=\p\Omega_{\ell}\setminus\Gamma,\qquad\Gamma_{t,\ell}:=\Gamma_{\!t}\cap U_{\ell},\qquad\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}:=\mathrm{int}(\Gamma_{t,\ell}\cup\overline{\Sigma}_{\ell}).

•

Let
k\geq 0
and let
S\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)
. Then by definition

S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Rot,\Omega_{\ell})

and we decompose by Corollary
3.4

S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}=S_{\ell,1}+\Gradgrad u_{\ell,0}

with

S_{\ell,1}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}^{k,1}S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})

and

u_{\ell,0}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}^{k,0}S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})
.
Lemma
3.9
yields

\displaystyle\varphi_{\ell}S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\ell}S_{\ell,1}+\varphi_{\ell}\Gradgrad u_{\ell,0}

\displaystyle=\overbrace{\varphi_{\ell}S_{\ell,1}-2\sym\big((\grad\varphi_{\ell})(\grad u_{\ell,0})^{\top}\big)-u_{\ell,0}\Gradgrad\varphi_{\ell}}^{=:S_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Gradgrad(\underbrace{\varphi_{\ell}u_{\ell,0})}_{=:u_{\ell}}

with
S_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})

and
u_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})
.
Extending
S_{\ell}
and
u_{\ell}
by zero to
\Omega

gives tensor fields
\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and

\widetilde{u}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
as well as

\displaystyle S=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\varphi_{\ell}S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{S}_{\ell}+\Gradgrad\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{u}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega).

As all operations have been linear and continuous we set

\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}S:=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}S:=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{u}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega).

•

Let
k\geq 0
and let
T\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)
. Then by definition

T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Div,\Omega_{\ell})

and we decompose by Corollary
3.4

T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}=T_{\ell,1}+\Rot S_{\ell,0}

with

T_{\ell,1}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}^{k,1}T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})

and

S_{\ell,0}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}^{k,0}T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})
.
Lemma
3.9
yields

\displaystyle\varphi_{\ell}T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\ell}T_{\ell,1}+\varphi_{\ell}\Rot S_{\ell,0}=\underbrace{\varphi_{\ell}T_{\ell,1}+S_{\ell,0}\spn\grad\varphi_{\ell}}_{=:T_{\ell}}+\Rot(\underbrace{\varphi_{\ell}S_{\ell,0})}_{=:S_{\ell}}

with
T_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})

and
S_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})
.
Extending
T_{\ell}
and
S_{\ell}
by zero to
\Omega

gives tensor fields
\widetilde{T}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and

\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
as well as

\displaystyle T=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\varphi_{\ell}T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{T}_{\ell}+\Rot\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{S}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega).

As all operations have been linear and continuous we set

\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}T:=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{T}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{\Div_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}T:=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega).

•

Let
k\geq 0
and let
T\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega)
. Then by definition

T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\symRot,\Omega_{\ell})

and we decompose by Corollary
3.4

T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}=T_{\ell,1}+\devGrad v_{\ell,0}

with

T_{\ell,1}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}^{k,1}T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})

and

v_{\ell,0}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}^{k,0}T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})
.
Lemma
3.9
yields

\displaystyle\varphi_{\ell}T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\ell}T_{\ell,1}+\varphi_{\ell}\devGrad v_{\ell,0}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\varphi_{\ell}T_{\ell,1}+\dev\big(v_{\ell,0}(\grad\varphi_{\ell})^{\top}\big)}_{=:T_{\ell}}+\devGrad(\underbrace{\varphi_{\ell}v_{\ell,0})}_{=:v_{\ell}}

with
T_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})

and
v_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})
.
Extending
T_{\ell}
and
v_{\ell}
by zero to
\Omega

gives tensor fields
\widetilde{T}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and

\widetilde{v}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
as well as

\displaystyle T=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\varphi_{\ell}T|_{\Omega_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{T}_{\ell}+\devGrad\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{v}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\devGrad\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\symRot,\Omega).

As all operations have been linear and continuous we set

\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1}T:=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{T}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{T}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,0}T:=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{v}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega).

•

Let
k\geq 1
and let
S\in\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
. Then by definition

S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\divDiv,\Omega_{\ell})

and we decompose by Corollary
3.7

S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}=S_{\ell,1}+\symRot T_{\ell,0}

with

S_{\ell,1}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}^{k,k-1,1}S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})

and

T_{\ell,0}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}^{k,k-1,0}S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})
.
Thus

(18)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \varphi_{\ell}S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}&=\varphi_{\ell}S_{\ell,1}+\varphi_{\ell}\symRot T_{\ell,0}\\
&=\underbrace{\varphi_{\ell}S_{\ell,1}+\sym(T_{\ell,0}\spn\grad\varphi_{\ell})}_{=:S_{\ell}}+\symRot(\underbrace{\varphi_{\ell}T_{\ell,0})}_{=:T_{\ell}}\end{aligned}

with
S_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})

and
T_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})
.
Extending
S_{\ell}
and
T_{\ell}
by zero to
\Omega

gives fields
\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and

\widetilde{T}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
as well as

\displaystyle S=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\varphi_{\ell}S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{S}_{\ell}+\symRot\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{T}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\subset\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega).

As all operations have been linear and continuous we set

\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,1}S:=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,k-1,0}S:=\sum_{\ell=-L}^{L}\widetilde{T}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega).

•

Let
k\geq 0
and let
S\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
. Then by definition

S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\mbH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\divDiv,\Omega_{\ell})

and we decompose by Corollary
3.4

S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}=S_{\ell,1}+\symRot T_{\ell,0}

with

S_{\ell,1}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}^{k,k-1,1}S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})

and

T_{\ell,0}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\divDiv_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}^{k,k-1,0}S|_{\Omega_{\ell}}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})
.
Now we follow the arguments from (
18
) on.
Note that still only
S_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})
holds,
i.e., we have lost one order of regularity for
S_{\ell}
.
Nevertheless, we get

S\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),

and all operations have been linear and continuous.
But this implies by the previous step

S\in\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega).

Again by the previous step we obtain

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega),

and all operations have been linear and continuous.

It remains to prove the assertions on the operators
\devGrad
and
\Gradgrad
.

•

Let
v\in\mbH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\devGrad,\Omega)
. Then by Corollary
3.6

\varphi_{\ell}v\in\mbH^{k}_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\devGrad,\Omega_{\ell})=\sfH^{k}_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\devGrad,\Omega_{\ell})=\sfH^{k+1}_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell}).

Extending
\varphi_{\ell}v
by zero to
\Omega

yields
v_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

and
v=\sum_{\ell}\varphi_{\ell}v=\sum_{\ell}v_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.

•

Let
u\in\mbH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)
. Then by Corollary
3.6

\varphi_{\ell}u\in\mbH^{k}_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega_{\ell})=\sfH^{k}_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega_{\ell})=\sfH^{k+2}_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell}).

Extending
\varphi_{\ell}u
by zero to
\Omega

yields
u_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

and
u=\sum_{\ell}\varphi_{\ell}u=\sum_{\ell}u_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.

•

Let
u\in\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega)
. Then

\varphi_{\ell}u\in\mbH^{k,k-1}_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Gradgrad,\Omega_{\ell})=\sfH^{k+1}_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{t,\ell}}(\Omega_{\ell})

by (
7
).
Extending
\varphi_{\ell}u
by zero to
\Omega

yields
u_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

and
u=\sum_{\ell}\varphi_{\ell}u=\sum_{\ell}u_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.

The proof is finished.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.12
.

Note that these types of compact embeddings are independent of
\varepsilon
and
\mu
,
cf.
[
5
, Lemma 5.1]
. So, let
\varepsilon=\mu=\id
.
Lemma
3.10
(for
k=0
)
yields, e.g., the bounded regular decomposition

D(\A_{1})=\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)=\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Gradgrad\sfH^{2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

with
\sfH^{+}_{1}=\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and
\sfH^{+}_{0}=\sfH^{2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and

\sfH_{1}=\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)
,
\sfH_{0}=\sfL^{2}(\Omega)
.
Rellich’s selection theorem and

[
7
, Corollary 2.12]
, cf.
[
3
, Lemma 2.22]
,
yield that
D(\A_{1})\cap D(\A_{0}^{*})\hookrightarrow\sfH_{1}
is compact.
Analogously, we show the compactness of
D(\A_{2})\cap D(\A_{1}^{*})\hookrightarrow\sfH_{2}

using, e.g., the bounded regular decomposition

D(\A_{2})=\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Div,\Omega)=\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Rot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.16
.

We only show the representations for
R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})
and
R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})
.
The others follow analogously.

•

By Lemma
3.10
and Corollary
3.11
we have

(19)

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\Rot\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)=\Rot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega).

Moreover,

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}),

since by Theorem
3.15
(iv)

(20)

\displaystyle R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\sfH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}.

Thus it remains to show

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}\subset\Rot\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega),\qquad k\geq 1.

For this, let
k\geq 1
and

T\in\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Div,\Omega)\cap\mcH_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!n},\Gamma_{\!t},\mu}(\Omega)^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}}
.
By (
20
) and (
19
) we have

T\in R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\Rot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

and hence there is
S_{1}\in\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
such that
\Rot S_{1}=T
.
We see
S_{1}\in\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)
.
Hence we are done for
k=1
.
For
k\geq 2
we have

T\in\Rot\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)=\Rot\sfH^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

by (
19
).
Thus there is
S_{2}\in\sfH^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
such that
\Rot S_{2}=T
.
Then
S_{2}\in\sfH^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)
, and we are done for
k=2
.
After finitely many steps, we observe that

T
belongs to
\Rot\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)
.

•

By Lemma
3.10
and Corollary
3.11
we have

\displaystyle\divDiv\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\subset\divDiv\sfH^{k+1,k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)=R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k+1,k})

\displaystyle\subset\divDiv\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)=R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})=\divDiv\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega).

In particular,

(21)

\displaystyle R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\divDiv\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)=\divDiv\sfH^{k+2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega).

Moreover,

\displaystyle R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})

\displaystyle\subset\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}=\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}),

since

(22)

\displaystyle R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\sfL^{2}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}.

Thus it remains to show

\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}\subset\divDiv\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega),\qquad k\geq 1.

For this, let
k\geq 1
and

u\in\sfH^{k}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\Gamma_{\!n}})^{\bot_{\sfL^{2}(\Omega)}}
.
By (
22
) and (
21
) we have

u\in R(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}})=\divDiv\sfH^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

and hence there is
S_{1}\in\sfH^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
such that
\divDiv S_{1}=u
.
We see
S_{1}\in\sfH^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
resp.
S_{1}\in\sfH^{1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
if
k=1
.
Hence we are done for
k=1
and
k=2
.
For
k\geq 2
we have

u\in\divDiv\sfH^{2}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)=\divDiv\sfH^{4}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

by (
21
).
Thus there is
S_{2}\in\sfH^{4}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
such that
\divDiv S_{2}=u
.
Then
S_{2}\in\sfH^{4}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
resp.
S_{2}\in\sfH^{3}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
if
k=3
,
and we are done for
k=3
and
k=4
.
After finitely many steps, we observe that

u
belongs to
\divDiv\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
,
finishing the proof.

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.19
.

We follow in close lines the proof of
[
7
, Theorem 4.11]
,
cf.
[
3
, Theorem 4.16]
and
[
4
, Theorem 3.19]
, using induction.
The case
k=0
is given by Theorem
3.12
.
Let
k\geq 1
and let
(S_{\ell})
be a bounded sequence in

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
.
Note that

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)\subset\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\Omega)=\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma}(\Omega).

By assumption and w.l.o.g. we have that
(S_{\ell})

is a Cauchy sequence in
\sfH^{k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma}(\Omega)
.
Moreover, for all
|\alpha|=k
we have

\p^{\alpha}S_{\ell}\in\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)

with
\Rot\p^{\alpha}S_{\ell}=\p^{\alpha}\Rot S_{\ell}

and
\divDiv\p^{\alpha}S_{\ell}=\p^{\alpha}\divDiv S_{\ell}

by Lemma
3.18
.
Hence
(\p^{\alpha}S_{\ell})
is a bounded sequence in the zero order space

\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
.
Thus, w.l.o.g.
(\p^{\alpha}S_{\ell})
is a Cauchy sequence in
\sfL^{2}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)

by Theorem
3.12
.
Finally,
(S_{\ell})
is a Cauchy sequence in
\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma}(\Omega)
.
Analogously, we show the assertion for the second compact embedding.
∎

Proof of Remark
3.20
.

Let
(S_{\ell})
be a bounded sequence in

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
.
In particular,
(S_{\ell})
is bounded in

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)\cap\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}(\divDiv,\Omega)
.
According to Lemma
3.10
, i.e.,

\sfH^{k,k-1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\divDiv,\Omega)=\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\symRot\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)

we decompose

S_{\ell}=\widetilde{S}_{\ell}+\symRot T_{\ell}

with
\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and
T_{\ell}\in\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
.
By the boundedness of the regular decomposition operators,

(\widetilde{S}_{\ell})
and
(T_{\ell})
are bounded in

\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and
\sfH^{k+1}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
, respectively.
W.l.o.g.
(\widetilde{S}_{\ell})
and
(T_{\ell})
converge in

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
and
\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
, respectively.
For all
0\leq|\alpha|\leq k
Lemma
3.18
yields

(\p^{\alpha}S_{\ell})\subset\sfH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

and
\Rot\p^{\alpha}S_{\ell}=\p^{\alpha}\Rot S_{\ell}
. With the notations

S_{\ell,l}:=S_{\ell}-S_{l}
,
\widetilde{S}_{\ell,l}:=\widetilde{S}_{\ell}-\widetilde{S}_{l}
,
and
T_{\ell,l}:=T_{\ell}-T_{l}
, we get

\displaystyle|S_{\ell,l}|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\langle S_{\ell,l},\widetilde{S}_{\ell,l}\rangle_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}+\langle S_{\ell,l},\symRot T_{\ell,l}\rangle_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\langle S_{\ell,l},\widetilde{S}_{\ell,l}\rangle_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}+\langle\Rot S_{\ell,l},T_{\ell,l}\rangle_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}\leq c\big(|\widetilde{S}_{\ell,l}|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}+|T_{\ell,l}|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{T}}(\Omega)}\big)\to 0,

completing the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.29
.

Theorem
3.25
and (
15
) show

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=R(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S}},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k,1})\dotplus\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega),

\displaystyle\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)+\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega)

To prove the directness of the second sum, let

\sum_{\ell=1}^{d_{\Omega,\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}\lambda_{\ell}B^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}_{\ell}\in\Gradgrad\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)\cap\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega).

Then
0=\sum_{\ell}\lambda_{\ell}\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}B^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}_{\ell}\in\Lin\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}

and therefore
\lambda_{\ell}=0
for all
\ell

as
\pi_{N(\divDiv_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!n}}\varepsilon)}\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}
is a basis of

\mcH_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},\Gamma_{\!n},\varepsilon}(\Omega)

by Theorem
3.28
.
Concerning the boundedness of the decompositions, let

\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t},0}(\Rot,\Omega)\ni S=\Gradgrad u+B,\qquad u\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega),\quad B\in\Lin\mcB^{\prescript{}{\mathbb{T}}{\Rot}_{\mathbb{S},\Gamma_{\!t}}}(\Omega).

By Theorem
3.24

\Gradgrad u\in R(\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k})
and

\widetilde{u}:=\mathcal{P}_{\prescript{}{\mathbb{S}}{\Gradgrad},\Gamma_{\!t}}^{k}\Gradgrad u\in\sfH^{k+2}_{\Gamma_{\!t}}(\Omega)
solves

\Gradgrad\widetilde{u}=\Gradgrad u
with

|\widetilde{u}|_{\sfH^{k+2}(\Omega)}\leq c|\Gradgrad u|_{\sfH^{k}_{\mathbb{S}}(\Omega)}
.
Therefore,
